### Goal Alignment in LLM-Based User Simulators for Conversational AI
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.20152v1)
- **Authors**: Shuhaib Mehri, Xiaocheng Yang, Takyoung Kim, Gokhan Tur, Shikib Mehri, Dilek Hakkani-Tür
- **Abstract**: User simulators are essential to conversational AI, enabling scalable agent development and evaluation through simulated interactions. While current Large Language Models (LLMs) have advanced user simulation capabilities, we reveal that they struggle to consistently demonstrate goal-oriented behavior across multi-turn conversations--a critical limitation that compromises their reliability in downstream applications. We introduce User Goal State Tracking (UGST), a novel framework that tracks user goal progression throughout conversations. Leveraging UGST, we present a three-stage methodology for developing user simulators that can autonomously track goal progression and reason to generate goal-aligned responses. Moreover, we establish comprehensive evaluation metrics for measuring goal alignment in user simulators, and demonstrate that our approach yields substantial improvements across two benchmarks (MultiWOZ 2.4 and {\tau}-Bench). Our contributions address a critical gap in conversational AI and establish UGST as an essential framework for developing goal-aligned user simulators.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the "goal misalignment problem" in LLM-based user simulators for conversational AI. Current LLMs struggle to maintain goal-oriented behavior consistently across multi-turn dialogues, limiting their reliability. The authors introduce User Goal State Tracking (UGST), a framework to track user goal progression, and present a three-stage methodology (inference-time steering, supervised fine-tuning (SFT), and Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) with UGST rewards) to develop more goal-aligned user simulators. They show improvements across benchmarks like MultiWOZ 2.4 and T-Bench, demonstrating that smaller models enhanced with their approach can match or exceed the performance of larger, unenhanced models. They also validate their UGST through human evaluation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in identifying and explicitly addressing the goal misalignment problem in LLM-based user simulators. While previous work has focused on improving realism or diversity, the authors' focus on goal adherence is a significant and relatively unexplored direction. UGST itself, as a dialogue state tracking adaptation focusing specifically on goal-oriented components, offers a novel perspective.  The three-stage training methodology is a well-structured combination of existing techniques (steering, SFT, and RL), but its application with UGST is what gives it novelty.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work stems from the crucial role user simulators play in developing and evaluating conversational AI systems. Inaccurate simulators can lead to misleading evaluations, hindering progress. By improving goal alignment, the authors are enhancing the reliability of user simulators, making them more useful for reinforcement learning, synthetic data generation, and agent evaluation.  The experimental results, showing significant gains in goal alignment, validate the effectiveness of their proposed methods. The fact that enhanced smaller models can compete with much larger counterparts underscores the potential for resource-efficient development.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and articulation of limitations in existing approaches.
    *   Well-defined framework (UGST) and methodology.
    *   Comprehensive evaluation with multiple benchmarks and human evaluation.
    *   Empirical demonstration of significant improvements.
    *   The ability of enhanced smaller models to perform at par with larger counterparts.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While UGST builds on dialogue state tracking principles, the implementation details of the LLM-based sub-component status update are not fully detailed and could benefit from further elaboration.  The prompts provided in the Appendix are helpful but don't give a complete picture of the implementation.
    *   The computational expense of UGST during inference, while addressed by SFT, remains a potential bottleneck for real-time applications without fine-tuning.
    *   The reliance on Qwen-2.5-72B-Instruct for UGST raises concerns about scalability, as it limits the use of the framework in more resource-constrained environments. The authors address this in the limitations section and suggest smaller models for UGST, it should be a topic for further exploration.
    *   The rewards in GRPO uses equal weights across all components, it might be interesting to investigate other weighting schemes,

*   **Potential Impact:** This work has the potential to significantly impact the development of more reliable and efficient conversational AI. By enabling better goal alignment in user simulators, it can improve the effectiveness of reinforcement learning and facilitate more accurate evaluations of conversational agents.  The proposed UGST framework could become a standard component in user simulator development.

*   **Rigorous Rationale:** The paper makes a significant contribution to the field by identifying and addressing a clear problem with a solid methodology and strong results. However, there are limitations that temper the excitement about the novelty and impact.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Trust the Model: Compact VLMs as In-Context Judges for Image-Text Data Quality
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.20156v1)
- **Authors**: Daulet Toibazar, Kesen Wang, Sherif Mohamed, Abdulaziz Al-Badawi, Abdulrahman Alfulayt, Pedro J. Moreno
- **Abstract**: Vision-language models (VLMs) extend the conventional large language models by integrating visual data, enabling richer multimodal reasoning and significantly broadens the practical applications of AI. However, including visual inputs also brings new challenges in maintaining data quality. Empirical evidence consistently shows that carefully curated and representative training examples often yield superior results compared to simply increasing the quantity of data. Inspired by this observation, we introduce a streamlined data filtration framework that employs a compact VLM, fine-tuned on a high-quality image-caption annotated dataset. This model effectively evaluates and filters potential training samples based on caption and image quality and alignment. Unlike previous approaches, which typically add auxiliary filtration modules on top of existing full-scale VLMs, our method exclusively utilizes the inherent evaluative capability of a purpose-built small VLM. This strategy eliminates the need for extra modules and reduces training overhead. Our lightweight model efficiently filters out inaccurate, noisy web data, improving image-text alignment and caption linguistic fluency. Experimental results show that datasets underwent high-precision filtration using our compact VLM perform on par with, or even surpass, larger and noisier datasets gathered through high-volume web crawling. Thus, our method provides a lightweight yet robust solution for building high-quality vision-language training corpora. \\ \textbf{Availability and implementation:} Our compact VLM filtration model, training data, utility scripts, and Supplementary data (Appendices) are freely available at https://github.com/daulettoibazar/Compact_VLM_Filter.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Trust the Model: Compact VLMs as In-Context Judges for Image-Text Data Quality":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a method for filtering image-text datasets using a compact, fine-tuned Vision-Language Model (VLM). The key idea is to leverage the inherent evaluative capabilities of a small, purpose-built VLM, rather than relying on extra modules or external APIs typically used in data filtration. The compact VLM is trained on a high-quality, annotated dataset (created with supervision from a larger teacher model, Gemini) to assess the semantic alignment and quality of image-caption pairs.  The paper demonstrates that datasets filtered by this approach, though smaller, perform comparably or even better than larger, uncurated datasets, suggesting improved training data quality. The authors also release their trained VLM, training data, and utility scripts for community use.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using a compact, fine-tuned VLM as a data quality "judge" has some novelty. While model-based data filtration isn't entirely new, the emphasis on a *small*, *dedicated* VLM, fine-tuned specifically for this task, is a distinguishing factor. This addresses the resource-intensiveness of using very large VLMs for data curation. A potential concern is that the method relies on a strong teacher model for annotation, which might limit its applicability if such resources are unavailable. The reliance on Gemini, specifically, raises questions about the reproducibility for researchers without access to the same API or resources.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the paper lies in its potential to streamline the creation of high-quality image-text datasets for VLM training. The increasing importance of data curation has been highlighted by many recent works. The paper provides a relatively lightweight and accessible approach compared to alternatives which often involve large models or human annotators. The release of the model and datasets is a valuable contribution that can facilitate further research in this area. However, the paper lacks a comparative analysis with other existing data filtration methods, making it difficult to assess the magnitude of the improvements. The evaluation is limited to CLIP-based alignment and perplexity scores, and the downstream captioning performance shows only modest gains, suggesting the benefits might be incremental rather than transformative.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The method is relatively simple and easy to implement, compared to more complex approaches.
    *   The use of a compact VLM makes it more resource-efficient than using large-scale models.
    *   The release of the code and datasets promotes reproducibility and further research.
    *   The paper highlights the importance of data quality over quantity in VLM training.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Relies on a large, powerful (and potentially inaccessible) teacher model for supervision.
    *   The performance gains, while present, are somewhat modest and require further validation across a wider range of tasks and datasets.
    *   Limited comparison to other existing data filtration methods.
    *   The use of specific evaluation metrics (CLIP alignment, perplexity) may not fully capture all aspects of data quality. A broader analysis, perhaps including human evaluation, would strengthen the findings.
    *   The ablation study only examines variations in score thresholds, not different components of the system.

* **Potential influence on the field:** The paper could influence how researchers approach data curation for VLMs. By demonstrating the feasibility of using small, dedicated models, it might encourage a shift towards more efficient and targeted data filtration strategies. The released resources could serve as a starting point for developing more advanced data curation techniques.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses outlined above, I believe a score of **6** is appropriate.

* The idea of using a compact VLM for data filtration is a helpful contribution, addressing resource constraints associated with data curation.
* However, the method is not radically different from existing data filtration approaches.
* The results, while promising, show only modest improvements in downstream performance.
* There is a lack of rigorous comparative analysis against other methods and a heavy reliance on a specific teacher model.
* Releasing the data/code is a significant benefit, and does raise the score.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### AnimeColor: Reference-based Animation Colorization with Diffusion Transformers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.20158v1)
- **Authors**: Yuhong Zhang, Liyao Wang, Han Wang, Danni Wu, Zuzeng Lin, Feng Wang, Li Song
- **Abstract**: Animation colorization plays a vital role in animation production, yet existing methods struggle to achieve color accuracy and temporal consistency. To address these challenges, we propose \textbf{AnimeColor}, a novel reference-based animation colorization framework leveraging Diffusion Transformers (DiT). Our approach integrates sketch sequences into a DiT-based video diffusion model, enabling sketch-controlled animation generation. We introduce two key components: a High-level Color Extractor (HCE) to capture semantic color information and a Low-level Color Guider (LCG) to extract fine-grained color details from reference images. These components work synergistically to guide the video diffusion process. Additionally, we employ a multi-stage training strategy to maximize the utilization of reference image color information. Extensive experiments demonstrate that AnimeColor outperforms existing methods in color accuracy, sketch alignment, temporal consistency, and visual quality. Our framework not only advances the state of the art in animation colorization but also provides a practical solution for industrial applications. The code will be made publicly available at \href{https://github.com/IamCreateAI/AnimeColor}{https://github.com/IamCreateAI/AnimeColor}.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the AnimeColor paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces AnimeColor, a novel reference-based animation colorization framework leveraging Diffusion Transformers (DiT). It aims to address the challenges of achieving color accuracy and temporal consistency in animation colorization, which are limitations of existing methods. The core contributions are: 1) integrating sketch sequences into a DiT-based video diffusion model for sketch-controlled animation generation, 2) introducing a High-level Color Extractor (HCE) for semantic color information and a Low-level Color Guider (LCG) for fine-grained color details from the reference image, which work synergistically to guide the diffusion process, and 3) a multi-stage training strategy to maximize the utilization of reference image color information. The paper claims superior performance over existing methods in color accuracy, sketch alignment, temporal consistency, and visual quality.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Strengths:**
    * **Addresses an Important Problem:** Animation colorization is a time-consuming and labor-intensive process, so automated solutions have practical value.
    * **Leverages a Promising Architecture:** DiTs have shown strong performance in video generation, and their application to animation colorization is a reasonable direction.
    * **Novel Architecture Components:**  The HCE and LCG modules are well-motivated and designed to address specific challenges in color control (semantic vs. fine-grained detail). The use of RADIO as a high-level feature extractor is a good choice, given the need for rich feature representations.  The Q-Former to filter FRADIO could remove the high-level noise from the high-level feature extractor.
    * **Multi-Stage Training:** The proposed training strategy helps to disentangle the various components and facilitates better training and performance.
    * **Comprehensive Experiments:** The paper presents both qualitative and quantitative comparisons against several relevant baselines. The visual results appear promising, and the quantitative metrics seem to support the claims. The inclusion of a user study strengthens the validation.
    * **Good Ablation Studies:** Ablation studies are used effectively to demonstrate the contributions of each component (HCE, LCG, the sketch conditional model and the use of RADIO versus CLIP).

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Incremental Novelty:** While the combination of techniques is novel, many of the individual components (DiTs, attention mechanisms, reference-based colorization, feature extraction) are building upon existing work. The claim of innovation is strong but, in fact, it is a combination of several technologies.
    * **Limited Technical Depth:** The technical details of the HCE and LCG integration are not thoroughly explained. The exact implementation and parameter settings for the attention mechanisms could be described in more details.
    * **Dependency on CogVideoX and training datasets.** The approach depends on the architecture of CogVideoX. The paper mentions they collected a large number of anime video clips. However, the source and detail of the dataset are missing.

* **Significance:**

    * The paper has the potential to impact animation production workflows. An improved animation colorization tool can improve efficiency and reduce the cost of animation.  The open-sourced code increases the impact of this work.
    * It combines multiple techniques in DiT and animation colorization to achieve the improvement of the overall performance.

* **Justification for Score:**

I'm assigning a score of **7/10**. Here's why:

The paper is a solid engineering effort that tackles a practical problem.  It combines existing techniques effectively and introduces some architectural components (HCE, LCG) that are well-motivated and designed. The comprehensive experiments demonstrate that the method achieves superior performance compared to the baselines.

However, the novelty is somewhat incremental. While the *combination* of the DiT architecture, the HCE/LCG modules, and the multi-stage training is original, the individual components are largely based on existing research. More detail about the integration of the various modules are needed to boost the quality of the paper.  The improvement over baselines is not dramatic (although it's consistent across metrics), and the paper might have greater significance if it showed a clear qualitative leap or enabled capabilities that were previously impossible.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### IFD: A Large-Scale Benchmark for Insider Filing Violation Detection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.20162v1)
- **Authors**: Cheng Huang, Fan Gao, Yutong Liu, Yadi Liu, Xiaoli Ma, Ye Aung Moe, Yuhan Zhang, Yao Ma, Hao Wang, Xiangxiang Wang, Yongbin Yu
- **Abstract**: Insider trading violations, particularly delayed disclosures of Form 4 filings, remain a persistent challenge for financial market surveillance. Despite regulatory requirements such as the two-business-day rule of the Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC), enforcement is limited by the lack of large-scale, labeled datasets and task-specific benchmarks. In this paper, we introduce Insider Filing Delay (IFD), the first and largest publicly available dataset for insider disclosure behavior, comprising over one million Form 4 transactions spanning two decades (2002-2025), with structured annotations on delay status, insider roles, governance factors, and firm-level financial indicators. IFD enables the first large-scale formulation of strategic disclosure violation detection as a binary classification task grounded in regulatory compliance. To demonstrate the utility of IFD, we propose MaBoost, a hybrid framework combining a Mamba-based state space encoder with XGBoost, achieving high accuracy and interpretability in identifying high-risk behavioral patterns. Experiments across statistical baselines, deep learning models, and large language models confirm that MaBoost outperforms prior approaches, achieving an F1-score of up to 99.47% under constrained regulatory settings. IFD provides a realistic, reproducible, and behavior-rich benchmark for developing AI models in financial compliance, regulatory forensics, and interpretable time-series classification. All data and codes are available: https://github.com/CH-YellowOrange/MaBoost-and-IFD.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces IFD (Insider Filing Delay), a large-scale dataset for detecting insider filing violations. This dataset comprises over one million Form 4 filings from 2002 to 2025, annotated for compliance and enriched with features like insider roles, governance factors, and market context.  The authors formulate the detection of strategic disclosure violations as a binary classification task and propose a hybrid model called MaBoost. MaBoost combines a Mamba-based state space encoder with an XGBoost classifier to achieve high accuracy and interpretability.  The paper benchmarked IFD against traditional models, deep sequence networks, and large language models, demonstrating MaBoost's superior performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the creation of the IFD dataset itself. While insider trading detection isn't a completely new area, the size, structured annotations, and regulatory grounding of IFD represent a significant contribution. The dataset fills a critical gap by providing a publicly available, large-scale resource for developing and benchmarking AI models in this domain.  The MaBoost model, although combining existing components (Mamba and XGBoost), presents a potentially novel architecture tailored to this specific financial compliance task. The ablation studies examine the contribution of various feature groups and model components. However, it's an incremental improvement on prior approaches rather than a radical breakthrough.

* **Significance:** The significance of this paper stems from the practical implications of improved insider trading violation detection. Timely and accurate detection can enhance market integrity, protect investors, and improve regulatory enforcement.  The IFD dataset provides a valuable tool for researchers and practitioners to develop and evaluate AI-powered surveillance systems. MaBoost demonstrates how sequence modeling and interpretable classification can be effectively combined for this task. A weakness is that it focuses mainly on performance metrics rather than exploring the qualitative aspects of detected violations or the ethical implications of such systems. While interpretability is mentioned, there's a limited demonstration of how the model's insights could be used in real-world regulatory auditing.

* **Strengths:**
    *   Large-scale, well-structured, and annotated dataset (IFD).
    *   Clear formulation of the problem as a binary classification task.
    *   A well-performing hybrid model (MaBoost) demonstrating the utility of the dataset.
    *   Comprehensive benchmarking against various baseline models, including LLMs.
    *   Ablation studies to analyze the value of various feature groups and module designs.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Incremental improvement on prior modeling techniques rather than a fundamental breakthrough in AI methodology.
    *   Limited qualitative analysis or real-world case studies illustrating the practical benefits of the system.
    *   Limited exploration of ethical considerations related to AI-powered financial surveillance.
    *   The reliance on financial experts for data authenticity is good, however more details on their qualification should be given.
    *   Lack of statistical significance or p-value is the models.
    *   Lack of details on the data filtering out of the records as this might cause a bias in the performance.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The IFD dataset has the potential to become a standard benchmark in the field, driving further research and development of AI models for financial compliance.  MaBoost provides a viable starting point for building practical insider trading detection systems. The paper could inspire the creation of similar datasets and models for other types of regulatory violations in finance and other domains.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:**

A score of 7 reflects the paper's significant contribution in terms of creating a valuable dataset and demonstrating a well-performing model for insider trading violation detection. The creation of IFD is a notable achievement. While MaBoost is an incremental improvement rather than a revolutionary AI innovation, it is nonetheless a tailored architecture that contributes value to the task at hand. While the limitations regarding qualitative analysis, ethical considerations, and the incremental nature of the modeling approach temper the score, the potential impact of IFD on research and practice justifies a score on the higher end.

- **Classification**: cs.CE
- **Score**: 7/10

### SGPO: Self-Generated Preference Optimization based on Self-Improver
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.20181v1)
- **Authors**: Hyeonji Lee, Daejin Jo, Seohwan Yun, Sungwoong Kim
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs), despite their extensive pretraining on diverse datasets, require effective alignment to human preferences for practical and reliable deployment. Conventional alignment methods typically employ off-policy learning and depend on human-annotated datasets, which limits their broad applicability and introduces distribution shift issues during training. To address these challenges, we propose Self-Generated Preference Optimization based on Self-Improver (SGPO), an innovative alignment framework that leverages an on-policy self-improving mechanism. Specifically, the improver refines responses from a policy model to self-generate preference data for direct preference optimization (DPO) of the policy model. Here, the improver and policy are unified into a single model, and in order to generate higher-quality preference data, this self-improver learns to make incremental yet discernible improvements to the current responses by referencing supervised fine-tuning outputs. Experimental results on AlpacaEval 2.0 and Arena-Hard show that the proposed SGPO significantly improves performance over DPO and baseline self-improving methods without using external preference data.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the SGPO paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SGPO (Self-Generated Preference Optimization based on Self-Improver), a novel LLM alignment framework that aims to address the limitations of traditional alignment methods that rely on human-annotated preference datasets and off-policy learning. SGPO uses an on-policy self-improving mechanism where the model itself refines its own responses to generate preference data for direct preference optimization (DPO). The key idea is to unify the policy and improver into a single model, trained to make incremental improvements to current responses by referencing supervised fine-tuning (SFT) outputs. Experiments on AlpacaEval 2.0 and Arena-Hard demonstrate SGPO's superior performance compared to DPO and baseline self-improving methods without using external preference data.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **On-Policy Approach:** The core novelty is the on-policy self-improving mechanism which directly tackles distributional shift issues that are present in off-policy methods relying on external preference data.
    *   **Unified Model Architecture:** Unifying the policy and improver into a single model is a smart design choice that allows the improver to be policy-aware and maintain on-policy behavior. This approach avoids the divergence between the improver and the policy that can occur in methods like SynPO.
    *   **Incremental Improvement & SFT Guidance:** The focus on gradual improvements guided by SFT data offers a more stable and effective learning process, as it avoids large preference gaps that can hinder optimization.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experimental results show significant performance improvements over DPO and SPIN on multiple benchmarks and across different base LLMs.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies effectively demonstrate the contribution of key design choices such as parameter sharing and the inclusion of improved responses during improver training.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on External LLM for Improver Training:** While the ultimate goal is self-generation, the initial improver training still depends on an external, stronger LLM (GPT-4). This dependency introduces some degree of external supervision into the process.
    *   **Complexity and Implementation Overhead:** SGPO introduces additional steps (improver training) and prompt engineering, which may increase implementation complexity and require more careful hyperparameter tuning.
    *   **Limited Iterative Improvement:** The paper shows the potential for iterative self-improvement by repeating Step 2, but the gains are not as significant, suggesting that more advanced iterative techniques might be needed to fully realize this capability.
    *   **Lack of Robustness to Domain Shift:** While the experiments are strong, the datasets are relatively similar in nature, so the robustness of SGPO to significant domain shifts isn't fully clear.
*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   The idea of using a self-improver for generating preference data is definitely a valuable direction.
    *   The unification of policy and improver model along with on-policy training is a practical and useful innovation.

*Score Justification:*

The paper presents a well-motivated and empirically validated approach to LLM alignment. SGPO addresses key limitations of existing methods with a novel on-policy self-improving mechanism and demonstrates strong performance improvements. While there's still reliance on a strong LLM for initially training the improver and the scope of the experiments could be broader, the paper offers substantial advancements to LLM alignment research. The ablation studies add strength to the overall paper as it proves the novelty and importance of each proposed change.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Diversity-Enhanced Reasoning for Subjective Questions
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.20187v1)
- **Authors**: Yumeng Wang, Zhiyuan Fan, Jiayu Liu, Yi R. Fung
- **Abstract**: Large reasoning models (LRM) with long chain-of-thought (CoT) capabilities have shown strong performance on objective tasks, such as math reasoning and coding. However, their effectiveness on subjective questions that may have different responses from different perspectives is still limited by a tendency towards homogeneous reasoning, introduced by the reliance on a single ground truth in supervised fine-tuning and verifiable reward in reinforcement learning. Motivated by the finding that increasing role perspectives consistently improves performance, we propose MultiRole-R1, a diversity-enhanced framework with multiple role perspectives, to improve the accuracy and diversity in subjective reasoning tasks. MultiRole-R1 features an unsupervised data construction pipeline that generates reasoning chains that incorporate diverse role perspectives. We further employ reinforcement learning via Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) with reward shaping, by taking diversity as a reward signal in addition to the verifiable reward. With specially designed reward functions, we successfully promote perspective diversity and lexical diversity, uncovering a positive relation between reasoning diversity and accuracy. Our experiment on six benchmarks demonstrates MultiRole-R1's effectiveness and generalizability in enhancing both subjective and objective reasoning, showcasing the potential of diversity-enhanced training in LRMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Diversity-Enhanced Reasoning for Subjective Questions":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the limitation of large reasoning models (LRMs) in handling subjective questions, where different perspectives can lead to varying valid responses. The authors observe that standard training methods (supervised fine-tuning and reinforcement learning) tend to promote homogeneous reasoning, hindering the exploration of diverse viewpoints. To counter this, they propose MultiRole-R1, a framework designed to enhance both accuracy and diversity in subjective reasoning. The framework involves:

1.  **Unsupervised data construction:** Generating reasoning chains incorporating multiple role perspectives.
2.  **Reinforcement learning via Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO):** Implementing reward shaping that considers both verifiable accuracy and reasoning diversity.
    Specifically, reward functions are designed to promote perspective diversity and lexical diversity.

The authors conduct experiments on six benchmarks, demonstrating that MultiRole-R1 effectively enhances both subjective and objective reasoning performance, and that there's a positive correlation between reasoning diversity and accuracy.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's core novelty lies in its focused attempt to explicitly promote diversity in the reasoning process for subjective questions. While multi-agent debates and ensembling approaches exist, MultiRole-R1 distinguishes itself by enhancing existing reasoning chains of individual models with multiple role-based perspective integrated in the reasoning trace. The integration of role-perspective chains and carefully designed reward functions within GRPO provides a unique approach to the problem.
*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the increasing interest in applying LRMs to subjective and open-ended tasks, which are common in real-world scenarios. Improving LRM handling of these tasks is crucial for their broader applicability.  The empirical finding that optimizing for diversity can *enhance* accuracy is a valuable insight.  The results on both subjective and *objective* reasoning tasks suggest the method's generalizability.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper articulates the limitations of current LRMs on subjective tasks and the root cause (homogeneity) effectively.
    *   **Well-Designed Framework:** MultiRole-R1 seems well-engineered, with logical steps in data construction, fine-tuning, and reinforcement learning.  The use of GRPO with carefully crafted reward shaping is compelling.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** Performance gains are consistently reported across multiple benchmarks, supporting the effectiveness of the approach.
    *   **Detailed Analysis:**  The analysis of accuracy-diversity correlations provides valuable insights.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Model Scale:**  The paper uses open source models R1-Distill-Qwen-7B, R1-Distill-Llama-8B, R1-Distill-Qwen-14B and Qwen3-8B. Scaling the proposed method to larger models like those of 70B is needed for greater applicability.
    *   **Role generation bias:** Relying on the model itself for role generation may introduce a bias towards the model's existing knowledge and prevent the exploration of novel perspective.
    *   **Data Construction Limitations:** The effectiveness of data construction depends on quality of data filtering.
*   **Impact:** The paper could influence the training paradigms for LRMs, particularly for applications where subjective reasoning and diverse viewpoints are important. This includes areas like chatbots, content generation, and decision support systems. The observation that optimizing for diversity improves accuracy will likely drive further research in this direction.

*The justification is based on critical analysis rather than mere summarization.*

**Score: 7.5**

The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of large language models by explicitly addressing and mitigating the problem of homogeneous reasoning in subjective question answering. The proposed MultiRole-R1 framework is well-designed, yielding strong empirical results and insightful analysis. However, limitations related to model scaling and role generation somewhat temper its overall impact. This score reflects a valuable contribution with clear potential for further development and influence.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### When Tokens Talk Too Much: A Survey of Multimodal Long-Context Token Compression across Images, Videos, and Audios
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.20198v1)
- **Authors**: Kele Shao, Keda Tao, Kejia Zhang, Sicheng Feng, Mu Cai, Yuzhang Shang, Haoxuan You, Can Qin, Yang Sui, Huan Wang
- **Abstract**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have made remarkable strides, largely driven by their ability to process increasingly long and complex contexts, such as high-resolution images, extended video sequences, and lengthy audio input. While this ability significantly enhances MLLM capabilities, it introduces substantial computational challenges, primarily due to the quadratic complexity of self-attention mechanisms with numerous input tokens. To mitigate these bottlenecks, token compression has emerged as an auspicious and critical approach, efficiently reducing the number of tokens during both training and inference. In this paper, we present the first systematic survey and synthesis of the burgeoning field of multimodal long context token compression. Recognizing that effective compression strategies are deeply tied to the unique characteristics and redundancies of each modality, we categorize existing approaches by their primary data focus, enabling researchers to quickly access and learn methods tailored to their specific area of interest: (1) image-centric compression, which addresses spatial redundancy in visual data; (2) video-centric compression, which tackles spatio-temporal redundancy in dynamic sequences; and (3) audio-centric compression, which handles temporal and spectral redundancy in acoustic signals. Beyond this modality-driven categorization, we further dissect methods based on their underlying mechanisms, including transformation-based, similarity-based, attention-based, and query-based approaches. By providing a comprehensive and structured overview, this survey aims to consolidate current progress, identify key challenges, and inspire future research directions in this rapidly evolving domain. We also maintain a public repository to continuously track and update the latest advances in this promising area.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "When Tokens Talk Too Much: A Survey of Multimodal Long-Context Token Compression across Images, Videos, and Audios" provides a systematic overview of token compression techniques specifically designed for multimodal large language models (MLLMs). The survey categorizes existing approaches based on the modality of the data (image, video, audio) and the underlying compression mechanism (transformation-based, similarity-based, attention-based, and query-based). It aims to consolidate current progress, identify key challenges, and inspire future research directions in this area. The paper also discusses the architecture of MLLMs, relates token compression to previous work in LLMs and vision transformers (ViTs), and outlines potential applications.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper claims to be the "first systematic survey and synthesis" of multimodal long-context token compression. While token compression and long-context challenges are individually well-studied, the specific focus on the *intersection* of these within the MLLM domain and the proposed structured categorization provides genuine novelty. Previous surveys primarily concentrated on LLMs in general or on unimodal compression. The organization by modality and compression mechanism offers a valuable framework. However, it's important to recognize that much of the component techniques are adaptations of methods from related fields. The novelty primarily resides in the *application* and *organization* within the MLLM space.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial bottleneck for deploying MLLMs in real-world applications: the computational cost associated with long context processing. Efficient token compression is essential for reducing latency and memory requirements, making this survey highly relevant to researchers and practitioners. By providing a structured overview of various techniques, the paper helps researchers quickly understand the landscape and identify promising avenues for further research. Identifying the connection between data-centric versus model-centric compression and emphasizing that token compression offers benefits beyond efficiency are insightful points. The discussion on the limitations of simply transferring LLM compression techniques to MLLMs is also crucial. The inclusion of a maintained GitHub repository contributes to the paper's practical value. The discussion of deployment challenges is also very important.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Coverage:** The survey covers a wide range of existing techniques in a structured and organized manner.
    *   **Clear Categorization:**  The categorization by modality and mechanism is logical and facilitates understanding.
    *   **Practical Relevance:** Addresses a key challenge in MLLM deployment.
    *   **Forward-Looking:** Identifies key challenges and future research directions.
    *   **Well-Written:** The paper is generally well-written and easy to understand.
    *   **Public Resource:** The associated GitHub repository enhances the paper's impact.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Critical Comparison:** While the paper provides a good overview, it could benefit from a more in-depth critical comparison of the different techniques, including quantitative comparisons when available across different modalities and tasks. The tables are helpful but do not tell the whole story.
    *   **Depth of Analysis:** While breadth is a strength, there could be more analysis of the *interactions* between compression methods and specific MLLM architectures. For example, how certain methods may be better suited to certain backbone models.
    *   **Generalization:**  The identified challenges and future directions are somewhat general. More specific, actionable insights derived from the current landscape could strengthen the paper.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field by:

    *   **Guiding Future Research:**  Providing a roadmap for researchers working on token compression for MLLMs.
    *   **Facilitating Technology Transfer:**  Helping researchers and practitioners adapt and apply existing techniques to new modalities and architectures.
    *   **Promoting Standardization:**  Encouraging the development of standardized evaluation metrics and benchmarks.

**Score:** 7.5

**Justification:**

The paper offers a valuable contribution to the rapidly evolving field of MLLMs. Its systematic organization and comprehensive coverage address a critical need for efficient long-context processing. The novelty, while not in inventing entirely new techniques, is strong in its specific application and organization within the MLLM domain. The limitations of a survey prevent a deeper dive into each technique but, by providing the first comprehensive survey of this intersection, the paper provides a solid foundation for future innovation and guides further development. The paper's potential influence on MLLM research and development is substantial, justifying a high score. The inclusion of a public resource is further value. However, the lack of more rigorous comparative analysis, limitations in the depth of architectural interactions, and somewhat general identified challenges keep it from a higher score. This score reflects the significant and timely value, as well as identifies key area for improvements.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Signed Higher-Order Interactions for Brain Disorder Diagnosis via Multi-Channel Transformers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.20205v1)
- **Authors**: Dengyi Zhao, Zhiheng Zhou, Guiying Yan, Dongxiao Yu, Xingqi Qi
- **Abstract**: Accurately characterizing higher-order interactions of brain regions and extracting interpretable organizational patterns from Functional Magnetic Resonance Imaging data is crucial for brain disease diagnosis. Current graph-based deep learning models primarily focus on pairwise or triadic patterns while neglecting signed higher-order interactions, limiting comprehensive understanding of brain-wide communication. We propose HOI-Brain, a novel computational framework leveraging signed higher-order interactions and organizational patterns in fMRI data for brain disease diagnosis. First, we introduce a co-fluctuation measure based on Multiplication of Temporal Derivatives to detect higher-order interactions with temporal resolution. We then distinguish positive and negative synergistic interactions, encoding them in signed weighted simplicial complexes to reveal brain communication insights. Using Persistent Homology theory, we apply two filtration processes to these complexes to extract signed higher-dimensional neural organizations spatiotemporally. Finally, we propose a multi-channel brain Transformer to integrate heterogeneous topological features. Experiments on Alzheimer' s disease, Parkinson' s syndrome, and autism spectrum disorder datasets demonstrate our framework' s superiority, effectiveness, and interpretability. The identified key brain regions and higher-order patterns align with neuroscience literature, providing meaningful biological insights.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces HOI-Brain, a novel computational framework for brain disorder diagnosis using functional Magnetic Resonance Imaging (fMRI) data. It focuses on capturing signed higher-order interactions (HOIs) among brain regions. The framework first employs Multiplication of Temporal Derivatives (MTD) to quantify dynamic functional co-fluctuations. Then, it distinguishes between positive and negative synergistic HOIs, encoding them into signed weighted simplicial complexes. Finally, it utilizes a multi-channel brain network Transformer to synergistically integrate lower-order edge features with higher-order topological invariants extracted through Persistent Homology (PH). The authors demonstrate the framework's effectiveness across Alzheimer's disease, autism spectrum disorder, and Parkinson's disease datasets, showing superior performance compared to existing methods. Key contributions include the MTD metric, the use of quadruplet interactions and 2D voids in PH, the distinction between positively and negatively synergistic HOIs, and the multi-channel Transformer architecture. They also provide interpretability by analyzing attention scores and identifying key brain regions and interactions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces several novel elements. The MTD metric for co-fluctuation is new. While PH has been used in brain network analysis, the extraction and differentiation of *signed* higher-order features *specifically quadruplet interactions and two-dimensional voids* are a novel utilization.  The multi-channel brain network Transformer for integrating lower and higher order topological features is also novel. Distinguishing between positive and negative synergistic higher order interactions is less common in the literature and a worthwhile contribution to understand brain dynamics.
*   **Significance:** The ability to capture more complex interactions than pairwise correlations is a significant step towards better understanding brain function. Demonstrating superior performance on multiple datasets (ADNI, TaoWu, PPMI, ABIDE) adds weight to its potential clinical utility. Providing interpretable insights, linking the model's findings to established neuroscience knowledge, strengthens its credibility.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Framework:** The paper proposes a well-integrated pipeline, addressing multiple limitations of existing methods.
    *   **Strong Experimental Validation:** Extensive experiments across multiple datasets and against a wide range of baselines.
    *   **Interpretability:** Analysis of attention scores and visualization of key regions provide valuable insights.
    *   **Novelty:** Introduces several novel approaches for feature extraction and modeling.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Computational Complexity:** While not explicitly stated, higher-order interactions often involve significant computational costs. A clearer discussion on computational efficiency would be beneficial.
    *   **Parameter Sensitivity:** The effectiveness of topological methods often hinges on parameter selection. While the paper discusses hyperparameter analysis for the number of clusters in readout later, a more in-depth discussion of the sensitivity of the PH parameters used and their effect on performance would be valuable.
    *   **Limited Discussion on Negative Results**: The paper finds negative synergistic quadruplets in AD, the rationale given is 'rely more heavily on negative information" with little discussion.
    *   **Dependence on specific fMRI processing pipeline:** The approach depends on a specific fMRI processing pipeline. A discussion of the robustness of the method to variations in preprocessing steps would enhance the paper.

*   **Potential Impact:** The work has the potential to significantly influence brain disorder diagnosis by providing a more nuanced understanding of brain network dynamics. The interpretable framework is promising for generating new hypotheses about disease mechanisms. The methodological contributions (MTD, the specific implementation of PH, the Transformer architecture) could inspire further research in network neuroscience and related fields.

*   **Rigorous Rationale** Overall, the research has several strengths and addresses important shortcomings of existing methods by utilizing Signed higher order interaction modeling and using multi-channel networks, increasing accuracy and interpretabiltiy for the use of AI for Brain Disorder Diagnoses. However, future work is needed to decrease the computational cost, parameter tuning analysis, and to further explore some of the interpretations of the findings.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: q-bio.NC
- **Score**: 7/10

### IQ Test for LLMs: An Evaluation Framework for Uncovering Core Skills in LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.20208v1)
- **Authors**: Aviya Maimon, Amir DN Cohen, Gal Vishne, Shauli Ravfogel, Reut Tsarfaty
- **Abstract**: Current evaluations of large language models (LLMs) rely on benchmark scores, but it is difficult to interpret what these individual scores reveal about a model's overall skills. Specifically, as a community we lack understanding of how tasks relate to one another, what they measure in common, how they differ, or which ones are redundant. As a result, models are often assessed via a single score averaged across benchmarks, an approach that fails to capture the models' wholistic strengths and limitations. Here, we propose a new evaluation paradigm that uses factor analysis to identify latent skills driving performance across benchmarks. We apply this method to a comprehensive new leaderboard showcasing the performance of 60 LLMs on 44 tasks, and identify a small set of latent skills that largely explain performance. Finally, we turn these insights into practical tools that identify redundant tasks, aid in model selection, and profile models along each latent skill.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "IQ Test for LLMs: An Evaluation Framework for Uncovering Core Skills in LLMs":

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a novel evaluation framework for Large Language Models (LLMs) based on factor analysis (FA), drawing inspiration from psychometric theory.  The authors argue that current LLM evaluations relying on benchmark scores lack interpretability and fail to capture the underlying skills driving performance.  Their method involves treating benchmark tasks as psychometric test items and LLMs as subjects, applying FA to model performance data to reveal latent skill dimensions (e.g., sentence comprehension, multi-hop reasoning).  They construct a comprehensive task-model leaderboard evaluating 60 LLMs on 44 tasks, then identify, interpret, and leverage distinct latent skill axes to construct a skill-centric leaderboard.  Finally, they demonstrate practical applications of their framework, including quantifying task novelty, characterizing skill profiles, and identifying effective LLMs for new tasks with limited data.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its application of factor analysis to the LLM evaluation domain. While FA is well-established in psychometrics, its use in this context is relatively new and offers a different perspective than traditional benchmark-based approaches. The idea of treating benchmarks as psychometric tests and LLMs as "subjects" is clever and provides a structured way to uncover underlying abilities.

*   **Significance:** The paper makes several potentially significant contributions:

    *   **Enhanced Interpretability:** The skill-centric leaderboard is more interpretable than a simple average score across benchmarks.  It allows for a better understanding of a model's strengths and weaknesses.
    *   **Task Design Insights:**  The analysis reveals redundancies in existing benchmarks and helps guide the design of future tasks by highlighting the novelty and relationship between tasks.
    *   **Improved Model Selection:** The framework enables more informed model selection by considering skill profiles rather than aggregate scores.
    *   **Practical Tools:** The provided analytical tools assist researchers and practitioners in task evaluation, model profiling and model selection, showing the method is actionable.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Analysis:** The study utilizes a large dataset of 60 LLMs and 44 tasks, providing a solid empirical foundation for the analysis.
    *   **Rigorous Methodology:** The application of FA is well-explained and grounded in established statistical principles.
    *   **Practical Applications:** The paper goes beyond theoretical analysis to demonstrate the practical utility of the framework with specific examples and tools.
    *   **Open Source:** The public release of code, leaderboards, and tools promotes reproducibility and further research.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Subjectivity in Skill Labeling:** While the authors automate skill labeling using LLMs, the process remains inherently subjective and requires human review and refinement. The labels could influence subsequent interpretations of the skill space.
    *   **Limited Model Scope:**  The study focuses primarily on open-source models in a specific parameter range, potentially limiting the generalizability of the findings to larger, proprietary models. It could be interesting to see if new factors emerge as models scale.
    *   **Task Coverage Limitations:** Although the task suite is extensive, it may not capture all possible LLM capabilities, especially in emerging or under-represented domains. The dependency on existing benchmarks also restricts the discovery of truly novel skills.
    *   **Reliance on LLM-as-a-judge metrics:** For generations tasks they are using LLM-as-a-judge which is not error-free (e.g., it is affected by position bias), it could make some analysis less reliable.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by encouraging a shift towards more interpretable and skill-aware LLM evaluation methodologies. It provides a valuable framework for understanding LLM capabilities and guiding future research in task design and model development. However, more detailed evaluation should be done on how does the new method influence future evaluation methods.

*   **Score:** 8

**Justification:**

The paper demonstrates substantial novelty by introducing FA to LLM evaluation. Its significance is reflected in the potential for enhanced interpretability, task design insights, and improved model selection. The strengths of the paper lie in its comprehensive analysis, rigorous methodology, and practical applications, the weaknesses withstanding, with skill labelling, model scope, and task coverage and limitations regarding the LLM-as-a-judge method. While the research is not without limitations, its potential to shift the field towards a more nuanced and skill-based approach to LLM evaluation justifies a high score. The public release of the code and data further enhance its potential impact.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Motion-example-controlled Co-speech Gesture Generation Leveraging Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.20220v1)
- **Authors**: Bohong Chen, Yumeng Li, Youyi Zheng, Yao-Xiang Ding, Kun Zhou
- **Abstract**: The automatic generation of controllable co-speech gestures has recently gained growing attention. While existing systems typically achieve gesture control through predefined categorical labels or implicit pseudo-labels derived from motion examples, these approaches often compromise the rich details present in the original motion examples. We present MECo, a framework for motion-example-controlled co-speech gesture generation by leveraging large language models (LLMs). Our method capitalizes on LLMs' comprehension capabilities through fine-tuning to simultaneously interpret speech audio and motion examples, enabling the synthesis of gestures that preserve example-specific characteristics while maintaining speech congruence. Departing from conventional pseudo-labeling paradigms, we position motion examples as explicit query contexts within the prompt structure to guide gesture generation. Experimental results demonstrate state-of-the-art performance across three metrics: Fr\'echet Gesture Distance (FGD), motion diversity, and example-gesture similarity. Furthermore, our framework enables granular control of individual body parts and accommodates diverse input modalities including motion clips, static poses, human video sequences, and textual descriptions. Our code, pre-trained models, and videos are available at https://robinwitch.github.io/MECo-Page.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary**

The paper "Motion-example-controlled Co-speech Gesture Generation Leveraging Large Language Models" introduces MECo, a novel framework for generating co-speech gestures that are controlled by motion examples. The key idea is to leverage the comprehension capabilities of large language models (LLMs) to simultaneously interpret speech audio and motion examples, allowing for the synthesis of gestures that both align with the speech content and preserve the stylistic characteristics of the provided motion. The method employs a three-stage fine-tuning process for the LLM, treats motion examples as explicit query contexts, and offers granular control over body parts. Experiments demonstrate state-of-the-art performance in terms of gesture quality, diversity, and similarity to the provided examples. The framework is versatile, supporting motion clips, static poses, videos, and text prompts as input.

**Critical Evaluation**

**Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its direct and effective integration of motion examples for controlling co-speech gesture generation within an LLM framework. Previous example-based methods often rely on pseudo-labels or vector embeddings that compress away kinematic details. MECo distinguishes itself by treating motion examples as explicit query contexts, thus enabling the LLM to learn the style characteristics from the provided examples in a more direct manner. Also, the multi-stage fine-tuning and the token-embedding intialization are important novel contributions to successfully train LLMs on multi-modal data.

**Significance:**  The ability to control gesture generation with motion examples has the potential to significantly improve the realism and expressiveness of virtual avatars and digital humans. MECo's ability to synthesize gestures that closely resemble the provided motion adds a degree of control that is not present in previous speech-to-gesture systems, making it easier for users to achieve desired stylistic characteristics.
The performance improvements reported in the paper, as evidenced by quantitative metrics like FGD and user studies, indicate that MECo represents a genuine advance in the field. The support for different input modalities is also a significant strength, increasing the framework's usability and applicability in various settings.
However, the paper's reliance on pre-trained LLMs also highlights a potential limitation. The results of training larger LLMs were not as good as with a smaller LLM. This highlights the reliance on data for this method to be successful. The paper states this potential shortcoming, making it less critical.
Finally, the paper identifies limitations concerning the kinematic realism of generated gestures (e.g., foot sliding).
The lack of a real-time implementation of the method can be a major drawback for using the method in many applications.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **7.5** to this paper.

**Strengths:**

*   **Effective use of LLMs:**  The paper convincingly demonstrates the benefits of leveraging LLMs for co-speech gesture generation, showing that these models can effectively learn complex relationships between speech, motion, and stylistic examples.
*   **Novel control mechanism:** Treating motion examples as explicit query contexts is a novel and effective approach to controlling gesture generation, leading to improved style preservation.
*   **Comprehensive evaluation:** The paper presents thorough quantitative and qualitative evaluations, including comparisons with state-of-the-art methods and user studies, which support its claims of superior performance.
*   **Versatile framework:** The ability to handle diverse input modalities (motion clips, poses, videos, text) makes MECo a versatile tool for gesture generation.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited realism in generated motions:**  Although the method achieves high levels of example similarity, there remains room for improvement in the kinematic realism of the generated gestures.
*   **Data Dependency:** The performance of larger LLMs are not as good as a small one.
*   **No real-time capability**: Since motion tokenizer is not causal, real-time applications will be difficult.
*   **Dependency on pretrained networks:** The method relies on a few different pretrained models which is a big constraint.
*   **Scope of applicability:** Although there are attempts to generalize motion beyond gestures, there is no rigorous comparison with methods which generalize motion with LLMs.

Despite these weaknesses, the paper's strengths outweigh its limitations. MECo provides a valuable contribution to the field of computer graphics and animation by introducing an effective and versatile framework for motion-example-controlled co-speech gesture generation. It is also very well-written. I do not feel there is much more that is desired in this paper.

**Potential Influence:**

MECo's approach to controlling gesture generation with motion examples has the potential to influence future research in the field. The framework's versatility and demonstrated performance may lead to its adoption in various applications, such as virtual assistants, gaming, and digital entertainment. The paper may also inspire new research on leveraging LLMs for multimodal tasks and on developing more realistic and expressive virtual characters.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 0/10

### CTR-Driven Ad Text Generation via Online Feedback Preference Optimization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.20227v1)
- **Authors**: Yanda Chen, Zihui Ren, Qixiang Gao, Jiale Chen, Si Chen, Xubin Li, Tiezheng Ge, Bo Zheng
- **Abstract**: Advertising text plays a critical role in determining click-through rates (CTR) in online advertising. Large Language Models (LLMs) offer significant efficiency advantages over manual ad text creation. However, LLM-generated ad texts do not guarantee higher CTR performance compared to human-crafted texts, revealing a gap between generation quality and online performance of ad texts. In this work, we propose a novel ad text generation method which optimizes for CTR through preference optimization from online feedback. Our approach adopts an innovative two-stage framework: (1) diverse ad text sampling via one-shot in-context learning, using retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) to provide exemplars with chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning; (2) CTR-driven preference optimization from online feedback, which weighs preference pairs according to their CTR gains and confidence levels. Through our method, the resulting model enables end-to-end generation of high-CTR ad texts. Extensive experiments have demonstrated the effectiveness of our method in both offline and online metrics. Notably, we have applied our method on a large-scale online shopping platform and achieved significant CTR improvements, showcasing its strong applicability and effectiveness in advertising systems.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper, focusing on novelty and significance:

**Summary**

The paper "CTR-Driven Ad Text Generation via Online Feedback Preference Optimization" (CTOP) addresses the challenge of generating effective advertising text using Large Language Models (LLMs) that maximizes click-through rates (CTR). It tackles the gap between offline evaluation metrics and real-world CTR performance. The authors propose a two-stage framework: (1) a diverse ad text sampling strategy using Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) with chain-of-thought (CoT) exemplars to increase diversity and quality, and (2) a CTR-driven preference optimization stage based on the Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) algorithm.  The DPO stage weights preference pairs using gain and confidence coefficients calculated from online A/B/n testing data, focusing on the CTR improvement over human-crafted baselines and the reliability of the data. The method is deployed on a large-scale online shopping platform and demonstrates improvements in CTR and revenue per mille (RPM).

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates novelty in its two-stage approach combining diverse sampling and a weighted preference optimization method for CTR improvement. Specifically, the use of RAG with CoT exemplars for generating diverse ad texts is an interesting way to leverage LLMs. The dynamic weighting scheme in the DPO framework, using both CTR *gain* and *confidence* (derived from a parallel AA test), is a significant contribution as it tackles the challenge of noisy online feedback effectively. The way the data from short-term A/B/n testing is used, in combination with AA testing to obtain a confidence metric for pairs, is a novel idea for dealing with the noise.

*   **Significance:**  The significance lies in the practical applicability of the method to a real-world advertising system. The reported CTR and RPM improvements are meaningful and demonstrate the potential for this approach to translate into increased revenue for online platforms. The paper explicitly addresses the shortcomings of directly applying LLMs for ad generation and offers a concrete solution. The framework's adaptability makes it significant, with the potential to be applied across varying ad situations. The inclusion of deployment details makes the results believable and relevant to practitioners.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Practical Focus:** The paper focuses on a practical problem and addresses it with a solution that is demonstrably effective in a real-world setting.
    *   **Addressing Noisy Feedback:** The dynamic weighting scheme in preference optimization is a strong point, dealing effectively with the inherent noise in online feedback data.
    *   **Clear Methodology:** The methodology is clearly described, and the experimental setup is well-defined.
    *   **Measurable Results:** The paper presents clear and measurable results (CTR and RPM improvements), providing strong evidence for the effectiveness of the proposed approach.
    *   **Well-defined research:** The paper identifies the problem, studies existing approaches, proposes new approaches that improve the state of the art, and validates the improvements.
    *  **Well-written:** The paper is well-written and easy to follow.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Baseline Comparison:** The baseline comparisons, while adequate, could be strengthened by including more recent state-of-the-art CTR prediction or ad generation models from academia. The absence of direct comparison with other comparable systems could be considered a shortcoming.
    *   **Data Sensitivity:** The sensitivity surrounding the business data limits the reproducibility of the study. This is understandable, but weakens the generalizability of the experiments.
    *   **Lack of Ablation Details:** Ablation experiments on the parameters of the gain and confidence functions could provide a deeper understanding of their impact.
    *   **Limited Qualitative Analysis:** While performance metrics are strong, a deeper dive into specific examples of how the generated ad texts differ from human-created ones and why they drive CTR gains would enhance the understanding.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper's approach to combining LLMs with online feedback optimization and its practical results are likely to influence future research in ad text generation and other similar areas where optimizing for real-world engagement metrics is crucial. It highlights the importance of addressing noisy feedback and adapting models to specific business contexts.

**Justification of Score**
The paper represents a valuable contribution to the field. It introduces a novel and practical approach to ad text generation that effectively bridges the gap between offline and online performance. While there are areas for improvement (as mentioned above), the demonstrated results and clear methodology make this a significant work. It showcases a real-world application with tangible improvements in a high-impact area.
